Nex Controller and Core API — 0.1.0

Implemented development API for administrator inventory, enrollment, durable operations, appliance adoption and managed-VM creation. Capability-gated storage/network registration, bounded ISO transfer and stopped-VM provisioning use outbound authenticated Core reports. Browser VM consoles use single-use tickets, an outbound host tunnel and a same-origin WebSocket. Central VM power requests (start, shutdown, reboot, force-stop, force-reboot) are durable host operations.

Download OpenAPI JSON. This reference works offline; examples do not execute requests.

refresh-inventory

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "payload": "None: only operation_id and kind. Created by POST /api/v1/hosts/{id}/operations with a stable request_id; at most one queued or dispatched refresh per host.",
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Read-only request for a fresh host inventory sample. Core journals it as pending without a managed payload and completes it succeeded when a probe inventory observed after admission is reported, or failed with 'fresh host inventory unavailable before probe deadline'. A pending refresh restarts its deadline after agent restart so it reconciles against a new sample. Delivered without the managed capability. A repeated operation_id is ignored as already admitted, including for legacy journal entries without a stored command; no effects are repeated."
}

pool-member-join

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/PoolCommand"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Reserved master-directed pool command. Core refuses it because membership is admitted only through the authenticated one-use Core-peer exchange; Controller cannot create or replace membership."
}

pool-storage-reconcile

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/PoolCommand"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Master-directed reconciliation for one NFS or SMB repository. The addressed member pulls the master record over pinned TLS 1.3 peer mTLS, stores an SMB secret only in its own Core credential state, journals native preparation, verifies QEMU access and a cross-Core non-blocking lock, then posts its generation-bound access observation. Before member VM/media work it reserves the current repository generation at the master; failed local work releases that reservation, while a durable definition retains it until the healthy serialized member journal proves it unused. Master source/credential/detach mutations write a Core-owned marker and fail closed while reservations exist. Local ZFS, ordinary iSCSI and generic native disks remain local and are refused/no fencing applies. Controller does not authorize membership, receive SMB secret bytes, or drive retries.",
  "peer_lock": {
    "transport": "POST/DELETE /api/v1/pool/storage/{repository_id}/lock on the Core-peer listener",
    "security": "Pinned TLS 1.3 mTLS; current authenticated pool member. POST checks repository eligibility and generation. Lease ownership is bound to the authenticated member, repository, generation, challenge and mode.",
    "parameters": {
      "generation": "Current repository generation",
      "challenge": "32 hexadecimal characters, generated randomly per attempt",
      "probe_mode": "Optional challenge-byte-v1; absent means legacy byte zero"
    },
    "acknowledgement": "204; challenge-byte-v1 POST echoes X-Nex-Pool-Probe-Mode: challenge-byte-v1. Missing echo selects legacy byte zero; a different nonempty echo fails closed.",
    "semantics": "A nonzero one-byte OFD range derived from the challenge on .nex-peer-lock isolates simultaneous proofs. Only EAGAIN/EACCES establish conflict; after DELETE the member must acquire the same range. A rare range collision fails closed. Unknown modes and malformed challenges are rejected; unavailable generation or lock returns failure. Leases expire after ten seconds and are not VM ownership or fencing authority.",
    "compatibility": "New master supports legacy byte-zero requests. New member falls back to byte zero when an old master omits the acknowledgement, retaining the legacy contention limitation. Core retries periodically with a fresh challenge; no Controller retry job or new durable state.",
    "offset": "SHA-256 of decoded 16-byte challenge; big-endian uint64 of first eight digest bytes, AND ((1<<63)-2), then add one. Byte zero is reserved for legacy peers.",
    "lease_limit": {
      "active": 64,
      "at_capacity": "New leases return lock-unavailable conflict; exact held-lease replay remains idempotent. Release or ten-second expiry frees capacity."
    }
  }
}

storage-register

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "managed-vm-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/StorageRegistration"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Verify existing local directory access; no formatting. Refuse, as a failed result, a path another non-failed registration on this host already holds. The host self-registers /var/lib/libvirt/images as ISO through this same verification, with a host-generated ID, unless any journal entry already holds that path. Succeeded registrations are reported back in Inventory.managed.storage and adopted by a Controller that does not hold them (request_id adopt-storage:<host>:<id>) without redelivery."
}

storage-update

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "storage-repositories-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/StorageLifecycleRequest"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "A name-only update is metadata-only and preserves credentials without reconnecting. Source changes and credential rotation are guarded: rotation requires all referring VMs shut off and no pending or needs_attention disk/media/ISO work. Core compares generation, journals intent before effects, reports needs_attention if rollback is uncertain, and never returns or journals credential bytes."
}

storage-recheck

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "storage-repositories-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/StorageLifecycleRequest"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Core observes the registered source without changing configuration or credentials. A missing, replaced, readonly or inaccessible backing source is reported and blocks future disk/media writes; it never uses an empty mountpoint. An identical Controller replay only returns the original operation. Core separately retries at most one due pending/needs_attention storage journal entry per minute in its serialized managed-worker lane, retaining the original result and excluding definitive failures; this continues through Controller loss."
}

storage-detach

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "storage-repositories-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/StorageLifecycleRequest"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Core compares expected generation and applies the full dependency guard for VM disks, retained disks, ISO media and pending/needs_attention disk or media work. A success writes a tombstone and removes only Nex-owned access configuration; it never formats, erases, destroys a zpool or target, or deletes backing files. Uncertain rollback remains needs_attention."
}

    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Core rechecks that the opaque receipt is eligible and unreferenced before removing its local secret. A receipt already retired cannot restore or recreate a secret by replay. The command carries no credential bytes and Controller keeps no cleanup state."
}

network-register

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "managed-vm-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/NetworkRegistration"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Verify existing bridge or active libvirt network without edits; for kind vlan, idempotently create bridge <uplink>v<vlan> over the 802.1Q sub-interface, persist it under /etc/network/interfaces.d and refuse uplinks or VLAN devices already used by the host configuration. Refuse, as a failed result, a bridge (shared by kind bridge and vlan) or libvirt network another non-failed registration on this host already holds. The host self-registers <port> untagged for every bridge on a physical NIC or bond through this same verification, with host-generated IDs, unless any journal entry already holds that bridge. Succeeded registrations are reported back in Inventory.managed.networks and adopted by a Controller that does not hold them (request_id adopt-network:<host>:<id>) without redelivery."
}

iso-import

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "managed-vm-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Media"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Without media.url, fetch assigned staged media over host mTLS and verify supplied size/hash/ISO. With media.url, require iso-download-v1 and zero initial size/hash; Core downloads public HTTP(S) destinations with DNS/redirect address checks, no credentials or environment proxies, capacity limits and ISO validation. Core records computed size/hash in its durable applied journal, then reports Inventory.managed.media without URL. Controller mirrors completion metadata and a fresh Controller re-adopts identities and names. Interrupted dispatched work is reconciled through the journal; ambiguous imports require attention."
}

vm-create

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "managed-vm-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/VMCreateSpec"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Create a stopped VM from the full specification: one exclusive new qcow2 per disk inside the VM's own directory on each disk's storage target (sparse when thin, preallocated when not), one interface per NIC with its derived or manual MAC, per-device boot order, the memory mode's balloon and physical-backing configuration, the selected firmware and machine type, and libvirt autostart when requested. Refuse, as a failed result, a name another domain or non-failed journal entry on this host holds, an effective MAC (manual or derived) any domain or journal entry holds, and dedicated memory that, with what is already promised plus the 2 GiB host reserve, exceeds total host memory. When start_after_create is set, start the guest once after the definition is verified and record the outcome in the entry's start field; the creation result is succeeded either way. A retry or reconciliation never starts twice: an interruption at the start step is reconciled by observing libvirt (running means started, otherwise the start outcome reads 'interrupted before the automatic start completed'). A failed start keeps the VM defined and stopped and is reported as a separate outcome <operation-id>:start of kind vm-power, status failed, error 'VM created but not started: <reason>'. Reconcile journal and per-disk ownership on replay. Core resolves storage, network and media references by resource ID, not by pool/host ID, so creation works after re-enrollment under a new Controller. Succeeded definitions are reported back in Inventory.managed.vms and adopted by a Controller that does not hold them (request_id adopt-vm:<host>:<id>) without redelivery."
}

vm-power

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "vm-power-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/VMPowerRequest"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Issue one libvirt start/shutdown/reboot/destroy/reset for the named VM on this host. start and force-stop are idempotent against the observed state; shutdown and reboot are ACPI requests the guest may ignore; force-reboot maps to virsh reset, is running-only, and shares reboot's interrupt ambiguity; paused or transitional states are refused. The stage is journaled before the single request so an interruption is reconciled by observation only: succeeded when the observed state proves the outcome, otherwise needs_attention; never a second request. Queue-capable hosts (vm-power-queue-v1) persist FIFO command admission and check after_operation_id is a successful same-VM task before effects; no retry loop for genuine failures. Host-received queued commands survive restart/replacement and are reported as managed outcomes for passive adoption. Shutdown/reboot completion remains ACPI acceptance, not proof of guest shutdown."
}

vm-delete

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "vm-delete-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/VMDeleteRequest"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Undefine a stopped managed VM. Requires observed shut off. delete_disks=false retains qcow2 paths in the journal; delete_disks=true unlinks them after undefine. UEFI guests use virsh undefine --nvram. Succeeded deletes omit the VM from Applied.vms and emit DeletedVMs tombstones for Controller mirror removal. An interrupted undefine or wipe resumes when the same operation is delivered again: a domain still defined must be shut off (otherwise needs_attention 'stop the VM so its interrupted delete can finish; observed state is <state>'), undefine repeats with the same flags and a requested wipe repeats over the journaled paths, ignoring files already gone. A later vm-delete for the same VM settles the interrupted one only when delete_disks matches and then fails as already deleted; a different delete_disks choice is refused. Disks of a VM deleted with delete_disks=true no longer count as storage-detach dependencies."
}

vm-media

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "vm-media-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/VMMediaRequest"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Insert a verified ISO into, or eject media from, the named VM's CD drive on this host with one libvirt change-media (live and persistent). Insert attaches an empty CD drive first when the domain has none; eject on a drive that is already empty (checked against the live definition of a running VM) succeeds without any libvirt call and never attaches a drive. The resolved media path and the changing-media stage are journaled in one write immediately before the change, so an interruption before that write is simply re-applied and one after it is reconciled by observing the domain: succeeded when the drive holds the requested media (or none, for eject), otherwise needs_attention. Core records only media_id in the VM's journaled specification; boot order values are never rewritten because change-media leaves every device's bootindex as it is."
}

vm-update

{
  "transport": "POST /api/v1/agent/report response.commands",
  "capability": "vm-update-v1",
  "payload": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/VMCreateSpec"
  },
  "result": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"
  },
  "semantics": "Apply one revision of an existing VM's definition; refuse a revision other than current + 1. Classify the difference from the journal's current definition (NeedsStop): a shut-off guest is redefined after any new disks are allocated and a rename (domrename), with the same capacity, name and MAC conflict checks as creation; a running guest takes only device additions, removals and media changes, applied with attach-device/detach-device --persistent and change-media --live --config, and is refused before any effect for anything else; an added interface whose MAC is still live after an earlier removal the guest has not released is refused and the task fails before any effect. Disks are matched by name and keep their journaled target names; interfaces by MAC. Removed disk files are retained and listed in the journal. The persistent definition and per-disk ownership are then observed against the regenerated XML by device identity; an interruption is reconciled by observation, except a UEFI-to-BIOS change interrupted after its undefine (stage cleaning-nvram or defining), which Core finishes by confirming the domain is absent and defining the journaled BIOS XML, and which refuses to undefine a UEFI domain that reappeared. A stopped edit that renames the VM and changes Secure Boot runs the read-only Secure Boot checks first, then domrename, then the variable-store edit and define. The journal reports the highest succeeded revision as the VM's current definition."
}

GET /appliance-ready.json

{
  "description": "Appliance-only public JSON file served by the existing Controller UI file handler, not a separately registered API route. First boot atomically publishes it after initialization, verified local HTTPS UI HTTP 200, and guest bootstrap-secret cleanup. It must be absent from a pristine appliance image and may return 404 before preparation or on non-appliance deployments. A persisted marker records first-boot completion; it is not continuous health or successful host enrollment/adoption.",
  "security": [],
  "schema": {
    "$ref": "#/components/schemas/ApplianceReadiness"
  },
  "compatibility": "Host handoff requires bootstrap_version 1, status ready and a successful UI root response over the pinned Controller TLS identity. An older API-only Controller cannot satisfy appliance readiness."
}

GET /api/v1/session

Get administrator session

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Read-only; safe to retry. Returns CSRF token for same-origin mutations. All authenticated administrator operations have controller-wide scope. Does not extend the idle deadline. A 401 requires sign-in again; session JSON fields are unchanged and contain no expiry timestamps.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses)

POST /api/v1/session

Sign in as administrator

Validates existing administrator credentials and browser origin. Sets __Host-nex_session with Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/ and no Domain. Each login creates a new session; at most 64 active sessions per service. Sessions expire after 30 minutes without an authenticated mutation and eight hours absolutely; GET/HEAD polling does not renew them. Legacy unprefixed cookies are not accepted; sign in again after upgrade. Existing password hashes remain valid; new administrator bootstrap passwords must be nonempty valid UTF-8, at most 1024 UTF-8 bytes, without CR, LF or NUL; there is no minimum length or complexity rule. Login admission permits at most five valid-length attempts per one-minute actual peer-IP window, two concurrent password checks per service, and 4096 tracked peers. Forwarded client-IP headers are ignored. Login returns 429 when rate/concurrency/table or 64-session capacity is exhausted; bounded retries must not create an unbounded loop.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses)

DELETE /api/v1/session

End administrator session

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Invalidates the current session and clears its cookie. Repeated calls after logout require reauthentication.

GET /api/v1/sites

List sites

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Read-only; safe to retry. Results represent persisted controller state. Host connection freshness is separate from per-check health; stale inventory never proves VMs have stopped.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses) 
{

GET /api/v1/pools

List pools

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Read-only; safe to retry. Pools are never created by administrators: each enrolling Core host becomes the master of a new pool named after its hostname (master_host_id). The development Core local API provides an authenticated member join flow; Controller has no join UI or membership-mutation route and only adopts Core-reported membership. Master change and member eject remain unavailable. Results represent persisted controller state.

POST /api/v1/enrollment-tokens

Issue a site-bound enrollment token

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Creates a short-lived single-use secret bound to a site; the enrolling host becomes the master of a new pool in that site. Unknown site_id returns 400. Optional host_id binds re-enrollment to an existing revoked host whose pool belongs to this site (409 otherwise). Keep the returned token out of logs. Token issuance is immediate; never retry enrollment blindly after an ambiguous outcome. Token expires after ten minutes.

POST /api/v1/hosts/probe

Probe a host's local API TLS certificate

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Performs an insecure TLS 1.3 handshake to the given address (default port 9443) purely to capture the leaf certificate nex-core-local presents; it establishes no trust and stores nothing. The operator compares the returned fingerprint, subject and expiry against the physical host before confirming add-host. This never contacts the Controller-to-Core enrollment contract; it only inspects the host's existing local API listener.

POST /api/v1/sites/{id}/hosts

Add a host by address and root password

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Replaces manual token enrollment for a reachable host: the operator first confirms the certificate fingerprint returned by probeHost, then submits it here alongside the host's root password. The Controller re-verifies that same fingerprint with a constant-time comparison during its own outbound TLS handshake to the host — a mismatch fails the handshake and returns 502 before the password is ever sent. On a verified connection the Controller signs in to the host's local API (POST /api/local/v1/session), mints a fresh site-bound enrollment token (the same token contract createEnrollmentToken uses), and pushes it to the host's local enrollment endpoint together with the Controller's own served HTTPS certificate (the -tls-cert file, not the host-identity CA) as the trust the host pins for its Controller connection; the host then enrolls with this Controller through the existing agent-enrollment contract and becomes the master of a new pool in this site. The root password is used once for that single sign-in and is never stored, logged, or echoed back, including in error messages. Unknown site_id returns 400. Optional host_id re-enrolls a revoked host whose pool belongs to this site (409 otherwise, checked before any network attempt). A wrong password returns 401; a login rate limit returns 429 with the host's own message. If the host reports it is already enrolled under a different identity, this returns 409 with that host_id — retrying the same add-host call after a lost response is safe and resolves the same way: fetch inventory to see the enrolled host. Any other host-side failure returns 502 naming the controller_url the host was asked to reach, and any token minted for the attempt is deleted. If the Controller process has no served certificate configured, 503 is returned before any host contact; use manual enrollment.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses)

POST /api/v1/hosts/{id}/revoke

Revoke host credentials

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Immediately prevents host authentication. Does not stop guests, erase state, or remove local recovery. Re-enrollment requires a new token bound to this host.

POST /api/v1/hosts/{id}/operations

Request fresh host inventory

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. The only supported command is read-only refresh-inventory. Persist a stable request_id before sending; retry the same host/kind/request_id to recover the same durable operation. Poll operations for completion. Conflicting active work is serialized. Delivery may repeat after disconnect; agent persists completed results and reports them until acknowledged. This route remains inventory-only; managed creation uses its separate resource endpoints. No cancellation or central power operation exists.

POST /api/v1/agent/enroll

Enroll a Core host

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Outbound HTTPS; agent must verify Controller identity before transmitting its site-bound token. Provide a signed PEM ECDSA P-256 CSR and nonblank hostname (maximum 200 UTF-8 bytes, no CR/LF/NUL). Identity is host-owned: the host presents the host_id it generated and, as its pool's master, the pool_id and pool_name it keeps, and the Controller adopts them unchanged, so a replacement Controller recovers the pool with its original ID and name. host_id and pool_id must be canonical lowercase UUIDs (or the 64-hex form issued to hosts enrolled before host-owned identity) and pool_name a valid name, else 400. A fresh enrollment creates the pool in the token site with this host as its master, then records the host in that pool in the same transaction (audit pool.create and host.enroll); older agents that present no identity get Controller-minted IDs and a pool named after the hostname, which the response reports for the host to keep. A presented host_id already on record, or a pool_id already held by another master host, is 409: only a token bound to a revoked host may re-issue that host, and it must present that host and its pool (409 otherwise). A separate authenticated Core-local peer flow can subsequently join a member; this enrollment endpoint neither joins members nor changes master. The same key is the same host: a request whose CSR matches a recorded active credential (a retry after a lost response, even with a new token) returns that credential unchanged. Issues a 90-day host-specific client certificate and CA. The token binds to its first CSR; an identical token/CSR retry before token expiry returns the same identity without duplicate enrollment. A different CSR returns 409. After expiry or revocation obtain a new site-bound token; replacement requires explicit revoked host selection. Enrollment has no VM side effects. Compatibility: an older Controller rejects the new fields with 400 (unknown field); upgrade Controller and Core together.

POST /api/v1/agent/report

Report observations and reconcile commands

Implemented in development product 0.1.0. Outbound mutual TLS authenticates exactly one enrolled, unrevoked host. Versions 1 and 2 are accepted; version 2 starts repository negotiation without storage-repositories-v1, waits for the Controller storage_repositories acknowledgement, persists it, then advertises that capability. Clustered storage has its own v2 acknowledgement: a Core sends neither clustered results nor storage-clustered-iscsi-v2 until it receives storage_clusters_support with that exact capability. The Controller sends that acknowledgement on every v2 preflight. A newly reconnected Core may offer the session-monitor preflight while withholding storage-clustered-iscsi-v2; before its acknowledged follow-up report, Controller clustered routes return retryable 503 negotiation pending without dispatching work or falling back. Core resets this readiness on start, Controller replacement/enrollment and report disconnect, so an older strict Controller sees only the clean preflight; queued Core results remain durable until a matching Controller acknowledges. Persist a strictly increasing sequence across restarts; discard superseded reports. Null/omitted inventory is heartbeat only and never makes old checks healthy. Results refer only to this host operations; acknowledge persisted results before deleting local journal entries. Commands can redeliver and must be reconciled by operation_id. Controller reconnect requires fresh inventory; no automatic VM restart is permitted. Sequence range is 1 through 9223372036854775807. Superseded sequences receive 200 but do not update last-seen or inventory; operation results and command redelivery still reconcile. At most 100 results per report; result error is at most 4096 UTF-8 bytes. Results for queued, undelivered operations return 409, foreign/unknown operation IDs return 400. Terminal results are acknowledged without rewriting the original outcome. At most 100 pending commands return, in creation order. Inventory fields merge only when inventory observed_at increases; individual checks merge only when their observed_at increases. Controller derives per-check freshness independently from heartbeat freshness. Health changes are recorded as debounced audit transitions (default debounce five seconds, configurable). Reports advertise at most 32 capabilities of up to 100 UTF-8 bytes each. managed-vm-v1 gates new commands; omission prevents managed delivery and a newer sequence clears stored capabilities. Result states include needs_attention for uncertain host effects.

POST /api/v1/agent/rotate

Rotate a host certificate

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Outbound mutual TLS requires the current valid, unrevoked host certificate. CSR binds the next certificate to the same host. Persist identity atomically; retain recovery access on an ambiguous response. No VM side effects. Requires signed PEM ECDSA P-256 CSR. The new certificate lasts 90 days; previous certificates overlap for at most ten minutes. Repeating the same CSR while authenticated returns the existing unexpired certificate. After the overlap expires, use persisted new identity or explicitly revoke and re-enroll.

POST /api/v1/appliances/bootstrap

Create initial appliance site and enrollment intent

Administrator-only appliance bootstrap. Persist a stable request_id and random 64-character lowercase hexadecimal enrollment_token before submission. The same request, names and token return the original site without duplicates. Payload changes conflict. An authenticated replay refreshes token expiry to ten minutes without clearing a consumed token's CSR binding; consumed tokens support replay only of the already-enrolled agent CSR through the existing enrollment endpoint. No pool is created here: the appliance host becomes the master of an automatic pool when it enrolls. This does not create or start a VM. Core enrollment remains outbound verified HTTPS protocol v1. Revoked enrollment tokens are not recreated; use explicit recovery.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses)

The same request, names and token return the original site without duplicates. Payload changes conflict. An authenticated replay refreshes token expiry to ten minutes without clearing a consumed token's CSR binding; consumed tokens support replay only of the already-enrolled agent CSR through the existing enrollment endpoint. No pool is created here: the appliance host becomes the master of an automatic pool when it enrolls. This does not create or start a VM. Core enrollment remains outbound verified HTTPS protocol v1. Revoked enrollment tokens are not recreated; use explicit recovery.",

POST /api/v1/appliances/adopt

Adopt the existing Controller VM

Register the existing libvirt UUID with a separate host assignment and protected Controller role. The pool is derived from the host record; an optional pool_id must match it (409 otherwise) and the response always returns pool_id. Requires an active host and fresh successful libvirt inventory observing this VM. A UUID reported by another host is rejected, including revoked hosts and old observations: reconcile ownership first. Stable request_id and identical payload replay the original registration; altered payload or owner conflicts. Registration grants no fencing, power, deletion or migration permission.

GET /api/v1/vms

List managed VMs and protected Controller appliances

Unified VM listing. Existing adopted appliances preserve role=controller and protected=true without creation spec/status. Generic guest entries add spec and operation_id/status/error; spec is the complete VM specification (identity, CPU and topology, memory mode and ceiling, every disk and network interface, boot order, install media, autostart), and state/freshness always come from fresh successful libvirt observations independently of creation status. Read-only administrator access; safe to retry.

POST /api/v1/vms

Create a stopped guest VM

Administrator session required. Mutations require same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token; JSON rejects unknown fields and bodies over 1 MiB. Persist request_id before sending. Identical payload replays return the same resource/operation; changed payload conflicts. New work requires a fresh active host in the selected pool advertising managed-vm-v1. Poll resource lists and operations for terminal results. HTTP acceptance is not host completion. Shared bounds, enforced identically on Controller and Core: 1–8 disks of 1–2048 GiB on virtio, sata or scsi with names unique in the VM; at most 8 NICs on virtio or e1000e with unicast lower-case manual MACs; at least 1 vCPU with sockets × cores × threads equal to the count when a topology is given; at least 512 MiB in memory mode dedicated or maximum; uefi or bios; boot orders unique and positive across disks and install media (gaps are allowed, since ejecting media leaves one), with at least one boot device. Controller additionally caps CPU at 64 and memory at 262144 MiB, assigns the UUID and refuses capacity above the host's observed CPU or memory. The host decides everything its own record answers: VM name and MAC uniqueness against its managed journal and libvirt domains, and dedicated-memory admission against total host memory, are reported as a failed vm-create result with the reason (visible in Task history), not as an HTTP refusal. Storage, network and ISO must have succeeded on this host and pool. The install ISO may be on any verified storage on that host; it need not share a repository with the VM disks. Core journals effects and reconciles per-disk ownership before replay; needs_attention preserves uncertain resources for manual recovery. start_after_create is honoured by the host inside the same creation: once the definition is verified the creation has succeeded, and Core starts the guest once, recording the outcome in the journal entry's start field so a retry never starts it twice. A failed automatic start keeps the VM as a normal stopped managed VM and is reported as a separate failed vm-power outcome (<operation-id>:start, 'VM created but not started: <reason>') in Task history; start it from the Power controls. autostart is a libvirt property set while the stopped VM is defined. No console, overwrite, automatic cleanup of ambiguous disks or guest installation. Core requires 2 GiB available host RAM reserve and verifies KVM/x86-64 and machine-type libvirt capabilities; uefi requires advertised EFI firmware. Failed/uncertain VM names remain reserved; there is no retry/deletion endpoint. A non-empty drivers_media_id requires the host to report vm-media-drivers-drive-v1 (409 "the selected host agent does not support a driver disc drive; upgrade Nex Core") and a succeeded ISO import on the same host and pool (404 "the driver disc ISO is not registered and verified on the selected host and pool"); both are checked after the replay check.

POST /api/v1/storage/discover

Discover a storage source from Nex Core

Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. The Controller validates pool/host scope then relays one bounded discovery request only to the addressed authenticated Core advertising storage-repositories-v1 and storage-discovery-v1. Discovery is an observation, never a registration: it cannot create directories, mount or log in a source, retain iSCSI nodes/sessions, change repository state or journal work. The Controller keeps the request (including SMB/CHAP credentials when supplied) only in an in-memory request/response relay, deletes it on Core delivery, result, browser cancellation or the 30-second deadline, and writes neither credentials nor candidates to SQLite, a journal, audit log or cache. Core limits work to eight seconds and returns sanitized candidates only; no credentials, device paths, mount options or command output. A 200 result may be empty, failed or stale; 504 is stale when no Core result arrives before the deadline. Retry with a new request ID after timeout or cancellation.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses) 
{
  "operationId": "discoverStorage",
  "summary": "Discover a storage source from Nex Core",
  "description": "Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. The Controller validates pool/host scope then relays one bounded discovery request only to the addressed authenticated Core advertising storage-repositories-v1 and storage-discovery-v1. Discovery is an observation, never a registration: it cannot create directories, mount or log in a source, retain iSCSI nodes/sessions, change repository state or journal work. The Controller keeps the request (including SMB/CHAP credentials when supplied) only in an in-memory request/response relay, deletes it on Core delivery, result, browser cancellation or the 30-second deadline, and writes neither credentials nor candidates to SQLite, a journal, audit log or cache. Core limits work to eight seconds and returns sanitized candidates only; no credentials, device paths, mount options or command output.     },

      "description": "Controller persistence failure while checking host scope; no discovery was relayed."
    }
  }

GET /api/v1/storage/cluster

Read Core-owned clustered iSCSI status

Administrator session required. pool_id selects its current active master, which must have acknowledged and advertise storage-clustered-iscsi-v2. During a new Core's bounded v2 preflight, 503 means negotiation is pending: no status relay was sent, so retry after the acknowledged follow-up report. A Core that never offers clustered support remains 409. The Controller forwards one transient status request only after acknowledgement and retains no cluster configuration, job or secret. A 200 is the Core's current durable operation projection; a 504 means the Core did not answer within 30 seconds and is ambiguous, so retry this GET rather than assuming setup stopped. The Controller HTTPS write deadline is 45 seconds only to leave delivery margin for this existing 30-second relay contract; it does not extend Core work.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses)

  "operationId": "getStorageCluster",

POST /api/v1/storage/cluster/apply

Start Core-owned clustered GFS2 iSCSI setup

Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. The Controller selects the active capable pool master and relays this request only in memory. The Core persists and executes the job; a 200 only confirms the Core accepted/reported its current operation, not a healthy cluster. Same request_id with changed input conflicts at Core. Controller discards supplied mount/device paths and Core derives them from the validated iSCSI identity. The request cannot create formatting, arbitrary fence commands or Controller-held secrets. The Controller's 45-second HTTPS write deadline only leaves delivery margin for the unchanged 30-second relay deadline; it does not extend Core work.

POST /api/v1/storage/cluster/verify-fence

Ask Core to verify one cluster fence

Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. request_id identifies the destructive attempt; host_id is the target, and fence_confirmation must be exactly FENCE <host_id>. The Controller validates target capability and relays the confirmation transiently to the pool master. It creates no durable fence intent. An immediate accepted response is not a proof: Core records its durable cluster operation as verifying_fence with a request-bound unverified pending proof, and performs the actual exclusion observation only at the executor. Browser input cannot claim a proof. An early failed observation is uncertain. The unchanged relay deadline is 30 seconds; the Controller's 45-second HTTPS write deadline only leaves response-delivery margin. A timeout is ambiguous: the UI closes the dialog and disables that target until a successful Core status refresh, without automatic retry. A refreshed failed or uncertain proof may be manually reverified only with a new request_id and a new exact confirmation; Core retains the prior proof. A pending unverified proof remains disabled. This is existing recovery behavior, not a new interface.

POST /api/v1/storage/cluster/credentials

Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. The Controller retains bytes only through redemption/acknowledgement (or its 15-second expiry), then zeroes them; it persists neither a secret nor receipt. Apply later names only a secret-free credential request reference. Retry the same request_id after 503 to reconcile unknown delivery.

POST /api/v1/storage/{id}/update

Update a storage repository

Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. Queues an update after Core's current generation matches expected_generation. A name-only update is metadata-only and preserves the existing credential without reconnecting the backend for non-clustered repositories. A source update uses the full dependency guard. Credential-only rotation is allowed while attached only when every referring VM is shut off and no pending or needs_attention disk, media or ISO work refers to the repository. Generic updates, including a rename, are refused for source.iscsi.mode clustered_gfs2 as a failed Core operation: clustered iSCSI updates require a coordinated Core cluster operation. No coordinated cluster update API exists. Core revalidates source and receipt, journals before native effects, and reports generation/access. If rollback cannot be proven, the result is needs_attention, never a safe failed update. 202 is acceptance, not mount readiness; identical request_id replays and changed input is 409.

POST /api/v1/storage/{id}/detach

Detach a storage repository

Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. Core checks expected_generation and applies the full dependency guard, including VM disks, retained disks, ISO media and pending or needs_attention disk/media work. A successful detach removes only Nex-owned configuration; it never formats or deletes backing data. Generic detach of source.iscsi.mode clustered_gfs2 is a failed Core operation before native commands: clustered iSCSI detach requires a coordinated Core cluster teardown. No coordinated teardown API exists. If side-effect rollback is uncertain, Core reports needs_attention rather than a safe failure. 202 is not proof that unmount completed.

POST /api/v1/networks

Register pool guest network

Administrator session required. Mutations require same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token; JSON rejects unknown fields and bodies over 1 MiB. Persist request_id before sending. Identical payload replays return the same resource/operation; changed payload conflicts. New work requires a fresh active host in the selected pool advertising managed-vm-v1. Poll resource lists and operations for terminal results. HTTP acceptance is not host completion. No disk formatting. kind vlan creates an additive guest bridge on the host without touching the management bridge; other kinds perform no network configuration. Unobserved or non-physical uplinks conflict with 409. Definite failed registrations can be revalidated using a new request_id; queued, dispatched and needs_attention reservations must be reconciled first.

POST /api/v1/media

Upload and transfer a guest ISO

Administrator session required. Mutations require same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token; JSON rejects unknown fields and bodies over 1 MiB. Persist request_id before sending. Identical payload replays return the same resource/operation; changed payload conflicts. New work requires a fresh active host in the selected pool advertising managed-vm-v1. Poll resource lists and operations for terminal results. HTTP acceptance is not host completion. Send the raw ISO body, not multipart or JSON. Name must be a basename ending .iso. Controller hashes the actual stream, verifies CD001 at offset 32769 and stages privately before committing an iso-import command. There is no fixed per-image maximum: an upload is admitted only while the staging filesystem's free space, less a 256 MiB Controller reserve, covers it; one transfer admission at a time, 2-hour read/write budget. Capacity is bounded even with absent/false Content-Length. Identical retries reupload and hash the bytes before comparing the original request. Terminal import results release Controller staging; orphan files are removed at startup. Use POST /api/v1/media/download to request a public URL import on Core.

GET /api/v1/agent/media/{id}

Requires current unrevoked host mTLS identity and a dispatched iso-import operation assigning this media to that exact host. Other hosts see 404. Core fetches only its enrolled, TLS-verified Controller origin and refuses redirects. Streams private staged bytes with a two-hour write budget; Core checks expected size, SHA-256 and ISO descriptor before publishing read-only media. Missing staging returns 410; inspect the original operation before retry. No browser session or CSRF token is used on this agent route.

POST /api/v1/vms/{id}/console

Issue a single-use console ticket for a running VM

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Requires an administrator session, same Origin and X-CSRF-Token, and an empty JSON object body. The VM must be an adopted appliance or a successfully created guest, its assigned host must be freshly connected, unrevoked and report capability console-v1, and the VM must be observed running in fresh libvirt inventory. The ticket is bound to the requesting session user and VM, lives 60 seconds in Controller memory only, is delivered once to the host in its next report response and is consumed by the first matching WebSocket. The action is audited as console.open. No power operation is implied; a Controller restart ends open consoles without affecting the guest.

GET /api/v1/vms/{id}/console/{ticket}

Open the browser console WebSocket

Implemented in development product 0.1.0. WebSocket upgrade (RFC 6455, binary frames carrying raw RFB/VNC bytes for noVNC) authenticated by the administrator session cookie; the Origin header must equal the Controller origin and the ticket must be unconsumed, unexpired and issued to this session user for this VM. Controller waits up to 20 seconds for the host's outbound tunnel, then relays bytes both ways until either side closes. HTTP/1.1 is negotiated for this route; no other transport is offered. The ticket is consumed on first use.

POST /api/v1/agent/console/{token}

Attach the host's outbound console tunnel

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Core sends this over its enrolled, hostname-verified mTLS connection with Connection: Upgrade and Upgrade: nex-console after receiving a console request in a report response and dialing the running VM's local VNC UNIX socket. The token must name an unexpired session for this exact host that has no tunnel yet. The response is 101 and the connection then carries raw VNC bytes both ways until the browser or VM side closes. Other hosts, unknown or expired tokens and duplicate tunnels receive 404. No browser session or CSRF token is used on this agent route; Core never listens for inbound console connections.

GET /api/v1/updates

Check Controller software updates

Implemented in development product 0.1.0. Refreshes a signed Controller-owned APT index cache at most every 5 minutes and reports apt's candidate version for nex-controller against the installed version. The configured repo URL (NEX_UPDATE_REPO or /etc/nex/updates.conf, else https://nexvirtualization.com/apt) is written to that cache and verified with the packaged archive keyring; this keeps the unprivileged Controller service out of the system APT state. A refresh failure or package no configured source offers is returned in the error field with HTTP 200 so operators can still see the installed version. Administrator session required. Read-only; safe to retry.

POST /api/v1/updates/apply

Request Controller package update

Writes pending-update.json under the Controller state directory for the privileged nex-controller-update path unit. Returns 202 scheduled; the helper runs apt-get update then apt-get install --only-upgrade nex-controller, so dependencies resolve and the package signature is verified, and restarts nex-controller. A failed upgrade restarts the service before reporting. Returns 503 when apply-update or the path unit is missing. Controller cannot run apt itself (ProtectSystem=strict, NoNewPrivileges). Audits update.apply. Empty JSON body. CSRF and same-origin required.

POST /api/v1/vms/{id}/power

Request a VM power action on its assigned host

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Requires an administrator session, same Origin and X-CSRF-Token. Persist request_id before sending; the same id replays the same durable operation, a different action under the same id is 409. The VM must be an adopted appliance or a successfully created guest; its assigned host must be freshly connected, unrevoked and report managed-vm-v1 and vm-power-v1, and queued power requires vm-power-queue-v1. With this capability, a pending power/edit/media request becomes a same-VM prerequisite instead of a busy refusal. Core persists FIFO admission order and executes after the prerequisite succeeds; failed, uncertain or missing prerequisites fail before effects. Without queue capability another pending task is 409. The request is audited as vm-power and delivered to the host in its next report; poll operations for the result. A succeeded shutdown or reboot means the ACPI request was issued, not that the guest honored it: the VM's observed state is the truth. Controller outage never stops a guest. Core-received commands survive agent restart and Controller replacement. Inventory.managed.outcomes exposes queued and terminal tasks for passive adoption without redispatch. Queue order concerns task completion, not guest ACPI completion; actual failures are not automatically retried.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses)

  "operationId": "powerVM",

POST /api/v1/hosts/{id}/console

Issue a single-use console ticket for a host

Requires an administrator session, same Origin and X-CSRF-Token, and an empty JSON object body. The host must be freshly connected, unrevoked and report capability host-console-v1 (distinct from console-v1, which only ever covers VM consoles; an agent without host-console-v1 gets 409 rather than a ticket it can never fulfil). The ticket is bound to the requesting session user and host, lives 60 seconds in Controller memory only, is delivered once to the host in its next report response with an empty vm_id (host console), and is consumed by the first matching WebSocket. Audited as host.console.open.

POST /api/v1/vms/{id}/media

Insert or eject ISO media on a VM CDROM

Queues a durable vm-media command on the VM assigned host. Requires administrator session, CSRF, stable request_id, action insert|eject, and media_id for insert of a verified ISO on that host. Host must report vm-media-v1. Both actions apply to a running VM, on pool-shared storage too: the record changes media_id only, so no boot order changes and no stop is required. Audited; operation appears in the operations list. Optional drive selects install (default) or drivers; drivers requires vm-media-drivers-drive-v1.

PUT /api/v1/vms/{id}

Replace a VM's definition on its assigned host

Implemented in development product 0.1.0, protocol v1. Requires an administrator session, same Origin and X-CSRF-Token. Persist request_id before sending; the same id replays the same durable operation, a different body under the same id is 409. The body is the complete desired definition (the same shape as creation); Controller validates its shape and references like creation, refuses immutable changes (an existing disk's storage, size or provisioning), and, when the VM is observed running, changes that need it stopped (name, CPU, memory, firmware, machine type, any boot order, an existing disk's bus, an existing interface's network, model, MAC or MTU, or adding a SATA disk); interfaces are matched by effective MAC: when the request names any current interface by its effective MAC (as the VM list reports it, and as the bundled editor sends every existing interface), every interface with an empty MAC is new; when it names none, an empty MAC is matched by position and the request is refused with 400 if it removes, reorders or changes an interface at an existing index, while an unchanged interface keeps the current address at its position. Clients that send any existing interface's MAC must send all of them, or an omitted one is replaced by a new interface. Controller freezes every automatic MAC into the definition before its stop-first check, drawing new ones from the VM's derived range at the first index above the highest one the current definition uses that no requested or current interface holds, or the lowest free index when none above is free, so a new interface never takes the address of one removed in the same edit, nor, unless the highest-index interface was removed or index 255 is in use, of one removed in an earlier edit; then it queues a vm-update command at revision current + 1 for the host's next report. Name and MAC uniqueness against other VMs and dedicated-memory admission are checked by the host against its journal and libvirt, as for creation. Core applies live what a running guest can take (adding or removing VirtIO/SCSI disks and interfaces, inserting, swapping or ejecting the ISO, description, guest OS hint and autostart) and redefines a shut-off guest from the regenerated XML; removed disks are detached and their files retained, never deleted. While validating a running guest's edit, before allocating any disk or changing any device, Core compares the live and persistent domains and reports the vm-update operation failed, changing nothing, with "network interface MAC <mac> is still attached to the running VM; wait for the guest to release it or stop the VM, then save again" when an added interface's MAC is still on a live interface that the guest has not released, changing nothing. The host reports the new definition as current and Controller refreshes its mirror from that report; poll operations for the result. One vm-update, vm-power or vm-media request per VM may be in flight.

DELETE /api/v1/vms/{id}

Delete a stopped guest VM on its assigned host

Implemented in development product 0.2.6, protocol v1. Requires an administrator session, same Origin and X-CSRF-Token. Persist request_id before sending; the same id replays the same durable operation, a different body under the same id is 409. Body delete_disks=false retains guest disk files on storage after undefine; delete_disks=true unlinks them after undefine. Requires observed shut off with fresh host report, vm-delete-v1 on the host, and refuses the Controller appliance. One vm-update, vm-power, vm-media or vm-delete request per VM may be in flight. Core journals the delete, reports a DeletedVMs tombstone, and Controller drops its vm-create mirror. Poll operations for the result; HTTP 202 is not proof the domain is gone. A new delete for a VM whose earlier delete was interrupted finishes that earlier delete when both requests make the same delete_disks choice, then fails with 'VM is already deleted on this host: an earlier interrupted delete has now finished' (or 'an earlier interrupted delete for this VM is still unresolved: <reason>'); with a different delete_disks choice Core refuses the new request without finishing the earlier one, so disks a later request asked to keep are never wiped.

POST /api/v1/media/download

Download a public ISO URL on Nex Core

Administrator session, same HTTPS Origin and X-CSRF-Token required. JSON rejects unknown fields and bodies over 1 MiB. Supply a stable request_id, verified storage_id, .iso basename name and public HTTP(S) url (at most 4096 characters; no userinfo or fragment). Controller queues iso-import on the selected storage host; it never fetches or stages URL bytes. New requests require a fresh active host with managed-vm-v1 and iso-download-v1. Identical retries return the original operation even while the host is unavailable; changed payload returns 409. Core validates public destinations on every DNS lookup and redirect without Controller credentials or environment proxies, bounds streaming to destination free space with a reserve, verifies ISO9660 and computes size/SHA-256 before publishing. Initial size_bytes=0 and sha256 empty; successful host inventory supplies verified metadata. URL is omitted from client responses and inventory. Poll media and operations for completion. Host journal owns download recovery; ambiguous interrupted imports require attention. Older hosts do not receive URL commands; uploading a local file remains available.

DELETE /api/v1/media/{id}

Remove a failed ISO import from the library

Implemented in development product 0.2.8, protocol v1. Requires an administrator session, same Origin and X-CSRF-Token. Removes the library record of an iso-import whose operation is failed or needs_attention, so a download that failed does not occupy the library forever with no way to clear it (BUG-0203). Retrying a failed download is POST /api/v1/media/download again under a NEW request_id; there is no separate retry operation. 409 when the import is still queued or dispatched, and 409 when it SUCCEEDED: the ISO then lives on its host, Nex Core has no media-removal command, and Controller will not drop a record for bytes it cannot delete. 409 while another media transfer holds the staging lock. 404 for an unknown id. Only Controller-owned staging bytes of a failed upload are deleted; no Core command is issued and no host state is touched. The removal is audited as media.discard.

GET /api/v1/vms/{id}/stats

Read a VM's usage history

Administrator session; read-only and safe to retry. Relays the query to the VM’s assigned host over a stats channel the host dials out (first query waits up to one report heartbeat, then the channel is reused and closes after 5 minutes idle). The history is recorded and kept by Nex Core; the Controller stores nothing. Requires the host to be fresh, not revoked and to report usage-history-v1.

      }
    },
    "404": {
      "description": "Unknown VM, or the host holds no history for it.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "409": {
      "description": "Host revoked, not freshly connected, or its Nex Core does not support usage history (upgrade Nex Core).",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "429": {
      "description": "live range refused: the host is already sampling its maximum number of objects every second (8). The host's message is passed through; retry later or use the 10m range.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "502": {
      "description": "Host answered with an error or a reply outside the contract; the channel is closed and the next query reopens it.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "503": {
      "description": "Host did not open a stats channel within 20 seconds.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "504": {
      "description": "Host did not answer within 10 seconds; the channel is closed.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

GET /api/v1/hosts/{id}/stats

Read a host's usage history

Administrator session; read-only and safe to retry. Relays the query to the host over a stats channel the host dials out (first query waits up to one report heartbeat, then the channel is reused and closes after 5 minutes idle). The history is recorded and kept by Nex Core; the Controller stores nothing. Requires the host to be fresh, not revoked and to report usage-history-v1.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses) 
{
  "operationId": "hostStats",
  "summary": "Read a host's usage history",
  "description": "Administrator session; read-only and safe to retry. Relays the query to the host over a stats channel the host dials out (first query waits up to one report heartbeat, then the channel is reused and closes after 5 minutes idle). The history is recorded and kept by Nex Core; the Controller stores nothing. Requires the host to be fresh, not revoked and to report usage-history-v1.",

POST /api/v1/agent/stats/{token}

Attach the host's outbound stats channel

Core sends this over its enrolled mTLS connection with Connection: Upgrade and Upgrade: nex-stats after receiving stats_channels in a report response. The token must name a pending channel for this exact host. After 101 the Controller writes HTTP/1.1 GET /stats?object=vm&id=<uuid>&range=<range> or GET /stats?object=host&range=<range> requests on the connection and Core answers each in order with a StatsHistory (200) or {"error"} (400 invalid query, 404 no history). Either side closes the connection on idle, restart or protocol error. Core never listens for inbound stats connections.

Complete operation contract (parameters, authentication, responses) 
{
  "operationId": "agentStats",
  "summary": "Attach the host's outbound stats channel",
  "description": "Core sends this over its enrolled mTLS connection with Connection: Upgrade and Upgrade: nex-stats after receiving stats_channels in a report response. The token must name a pending channel for this exact host. After 101 the Controller writes HTTP/1.1 GET /stats?object=vm&id=<uuid>&range=<range> or GET /stats?object=host&range=<range> requests on the connection and Core answers each in order with a StatsHistory (200) or {\"error\"} (400 invalid query, 404 no history). Either side closes the connection on idle, restart or protocol error. Core never listens for inbound stats connections.",
  "tags": [
    "Core \u2192 Controller"
  ],
  "security": [
    {
      "HostCertificate": []
    }
  ],
  "x-introduced-version": "0.1.0",
  "x-audience": "agent",
  "parameters": [
    {
      "name": "token",
      "in": "path",
      "required": true,
      "schema": {
        "type": "string"
      },
      "description": "Stats channel token from the report response."
    }
  ],
  "responses": {
    "101": {
      "description": "Channel attached; Controller-originated HTTP/1.1 queries follow."
    },
    "200": {
      "description": "Not returned; the channel is the 101 response."
    },
    "400": {
      "description": "Missing Upgrade: nex-stats.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "401": {
      "description": "Missing, expired, invalid, or revoked credentials.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "404": {
      "description": "Channel token unknown, expired, for another host, or already attached.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "500": {
      "description": "Connection cannot be upgraded.",
      "content": {
        "application/json": {
          "schema": {
            "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Error"
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

  "type": "apiKey",
  "in": "cookie",
  "name": "__Host-nex_session",
  "description": "Administrator session cookie __Host-nex_session, Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite=Strict, Path=/ with no Domain. Expires after 30 minutes without an authenticated mutation or eight hours absolutely. GET/HEAD polling does not extend idle lifetime; mutations also require matching Origin and X-CSRF-Token. At most 64 active sessions per service. Legacy unprefixed cookies are refused; sign in again after upgrading.",
  "x-session-policy": {
    "idle_timeout_seconds": 1800,
    "absolute_timeout_seconds": 28800,
    "maximum_sessions": 64,
    "idle_refresh": "authenticated mutations after Origin/CSRF validation; GET and HEAD never extend it",
    "legacy_cookies_accepted": false
  }
}

    "pool_name": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "Name of the pool held by the pool master, its hostname at first enrollment. Nonblank, maximum 200 UTF-8 bytes, no CR/LF/NUL. Defaults to hostname when omitted."
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "version",
    "token",
    "csr_pem",
    "hostname"
  ],
  "x-go-type": "protocol.EnrollRequest",
  "example": {
    "version": 1,
    "token": "<single-use-enrollment-token>",
    "csr_pem": "<signed-P256-CSR-PEM>",
    "hostname": "nex-core-1",

ReportRequest
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "version": {
      "type": "integer",
      "enum": [
        1,
        2
      ],

    },

      "type": "integer",

    },

      "type": [
        "array",
        "null"

        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Result"

    },

      "type": [
        "array",
        "null"

      "items": {
        "type": "string",
        "x-max-utf8-bytes": 100
      },
      "maxItems": 32,
      "description": "Advertised on every report. managed-vm-v1 enables managed command delivery. storage-cluster-session-monitor-v1 offers the additive clustered session-monitor projection; it must be acknowledged through storage_clusters_support before Core sends that projection. Omission or null disables managed delivery for this report; a newer sequence also replaces stored capabilities."
    },
    "storage_discoveries": {
      "type": [
        "array",
        "null"
      ],
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/StorageDiscoveryResult"

    },

      "type": [
        "array",
        "null"
      ],
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/StorageClusterResult"
      },
      "description": "Transient results for addressed clustered-storage requests, emitted only after the exact v2 storage_clusters_support acknowledgement. session_monitor_ready and monitor-stage members are included only after the offered storage-cluster-session-monitor-v1 extension is acknowledged. The result exposes Core-owned durable operation state but is not Controller state."
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "version",
    "sequence"
  ],
  "x-go-type": "protocol.ReportRequest",
  "example": {
    "version": 2,
    "sequence": 1,
    "results": []
  },
  "description": "Version and sequence are required by runtime validation. Results may be omitted or null and defaults to no results. Inventory may be omitted or null for heartbeat only. Clustered results and storage-clustered-iscsi-v2 are withheld until this Controller connection acknowledges storage_clusters_support. The additive session-monitor members are withheld unless the Core offered and the Controller acknowledged storage-cluster-session-monitor-v1. Both acknowledgements reset after a cluster preflight failure, reconnect or Controller re-enrolment. Version-one reports retain existing non-storage behavior.",
  "x-go-emitted-fields": [
    "version",
    "sequence",
    "results"
  ]

VMMemory
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "mode": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "dedicated",
        "maximum"
      ],
      "description": "dedicated preallocates and locks the ceiling in host RAM while running: memory and currentMemory at size_mib, no balloon, and memoryBacking with immediate allocation, locked and nosharepages. Stopped VMs release physical RAM but retain their Nex capacity commitment. QEMU overhead is locked too. Requires updated Core; existing VMs need full shutdown, a successful stopped vm-update save and start. Legacy dedicated live edits fail before device effects with a stop-and-save instruction. Upgrade, autostart or guest reboot alone does not rewrite old definitions. maximum caps without reserving: the same ceiling plus a virtio balloon with free page reporting, so the guest returns pages it frees. Reclaim requires the balloon driver running in the guest (built into Linux; virtio-win guest tools on Windows); without it the guest drifts to the ceiling and holds it. There is no pressure-driven reclaim daemon."
    },
    "size_mib": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 512,
      "maximum": 262144,
      "description": "Memory ceiling in MiB. Both sides require at least 512; Controller caps the request at 262144."
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "mode",
    "size_mib"
  ],
  "x-go-type": "protocol.VMMemory",
  "description": "Memory ceiling and how the host treats it. size_mib is the ceiling in both modes and host capacity preflight uses that ceiling in both modes: a VM that cannot reach its ceiling is refused. dedicated additionally counts against committed capacity; maximum reserves nothing and is allowed to oversubscribe."
}

VMNIC
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "network_id": {
      "type": "string",
      "minLength": 1,
      "description": "Registered network that has succeeded on this VM's host and pool, resolved by resource ID."
    },
    "model": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "virtio",
        "e1000",
        "e1000e",
        "rtl8139",
        "vmxnet3"
      ],
      "description": "Which network card the guest sees. virtio is paravirtualised: fastest and lowest host CPU, but the guest needs a driver (built into Linux, virtio-win on Windows). e1000 and e1000e emulate Intel gigabit cards nearly every OS already drives, for guests and installers that cannot see a virtio adapter, at higher host CPU. rtl8139 emulates a 100 Mbit card for guests too old for a gigabit driver; the limit is the emulated hardware. vmxnet3 is VMware's paravirtualised adapter, for guests already carrying VMware drivers."
    },
    "mac": {
      "type": "string",
      "pattern": "^(([0-9a-f][02468ace])(:[0-9a-f]{2}){5})?$",
      "description": "Empty derives a stable MAC from the VM ID and the interface index (protocol.DerivedMAC: the 52:54:00 locally administered prefix, two octets of sha256(id) and the index), which never collides across a VM's at most 8 interfaces. A manual address must be six octets in canonical lower-case colon form and unicast; the pattern encodes the cleared multicast bit as an even second hex digit. All-zero addresses are refused, addresses are unique within the VM, and Controller additionally enforces uniqueness across the pool by plain string comparison. The VM list reports the effective address, and an edit freezes every automatic address into the definition (drawn from the VM's derived range above the highest index the VM uses, or the lowest free index when none above is free), so interfaces keep their identity across later edits."
    },
    "mtu": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 1280,
      "maximum": 9216,
      "description": "Interface MTU, or 0 for the network's own. Accepted only when model is virtio: QEMU communicates an MTU to the guest through virtio's host_mtu feature and libvirt sizes the host tap to match, while the emulated models have no equivalent and would leave the guest on its own default. A value on any other model is refused rather than silently applied to the host side only. The floor is the IPv6 minimum MTU and the ceiling the largest jumbo frame common NICs accept; every device along the path must agree or traffic is dropped without an error."
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "network_id",
    "model",
    "mac"
  ],
  "x-go-type": "protocol.VMNIC",
  "description": "One virtual network interface on a registered pool network. A VM may have none and at most 8."
}
VMBoot
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "firmware": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "uefi",
        "bios"
      ],
      "description": "uefi is the default and requires EFI firmware advertised by the host's libvirt domain capabilities; bios stays selectable."
    },
    "machine": {
      "type": "string",
      "pattern": "^([a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,31})?$",
      "description": "Empty selects Core's default machine type (q35)."
    },
    "media_boot_order": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 0,
      "description": "0 keeps the CD drive out of the boot order; any other value joins the disks' contiguous 1..N numbering. Every VM has the drive, so the order is kept while it is empty and an ISO can be ejected from a running guest."
    },
    "secure_boot": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "",
        "on",
        "off"
      ],
      "description": "UEFI Secure Boot. Omitted means on with the Microsoft keys enrolled, which is exactly what libvirt already selected for every UEFI VM defined before this field existed, so a definition re-sent unchanged keeps the firmware its guest was installed with. off selects the plain OVMF build with no enrolled keys, for unsigned boot media or drivers. Requires uefi; on with bios is rejected. Core refuses the VM when the host's domain capabilities do not offer a matching firmware build, naming the setting rather than passing libvirt's firmware-selection error through. Changing it requires the VM to be shut off."
    },
    "tpm": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "",
        "none",
        "2.0"
      ],
      "description": "Emulated TPM. 2.0 adds a tpm-crb device backed by swtpm; omitted and none add no device, so a definition written before this field keeps exactly the devices it had. Windows 11 requires a TPM and refuses to install without one. Core refuses the VM when the host's domain capabilities do not advertise the emulator backend, rather than defining a device that cannot start. Changing it requires the VM to be shut off, and REMOVING a TPM is refused outright at any time: anything the guest sealed to it, including a Windows BitLocker key, would be lost, and libvirt cleans TPM state by iterating the devices the persistent definition still carries, so a redefine without it would orphan that state."
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "firmware",
    "machine",
    "media_boot_order"
  ],
  "x-go-type": "protocol.VMBoot",
  "description": "Firmware, Secure Boot, TPM, machine type and the install media's place in the boot order. Boot order is per device (libvirt forbids mixing per-device boot with an os/boot device list), so at least one disk or the install media must carry a non-zero order."
}

    },
    "nics": {
      "type": [
        "array",
        "null"
      ],
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/VMNIC"
      },
      "maxItems": 8,
      "description": "Zero to eight network interfaces."
    },
    "boot": {
      "$ref": "#/components/schemas/VMBoot"
    },
    "media_id": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "Install ISO (Media.id), empty for none. The ISO must be imported and verified on the placement host; it may live on any storage that host can read and does not need to share a repository with the VM's disks. Every VM has the CD drive, so a non-zero boot.media_boot_order does not require media_id: an emptied drive keeps its place in the boot order."
    },
    "drivers_media_id": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "ISO in the VM's second CD drive, for a driver disc an installer needs alongside its install media - a Windows guest installing to a VirtIO disk needs the virtio-win ISO here. Empty leaves that drive empty; the drive itself always exists on a host reporting vm-media-drivers-drive-v1. Must differ from media_id."
    },
    "start_after_create": {
      "type": "boolean",
      "description": "Honoured by the host inside the creation, after the definition is verified, so the creation itself has succeeded whether or not the flag is set: Core starts the guest exactly once and records the outcome in the journal entry's start field. A failed automatic start leaves the VM defined and stopped as a normal managed VM (power, console and media work; a replacement Controller adopts it) and is reported by the host as its own outcome with ID <operation-id>:start, kind vm-power, status failed and error 'VM created but not started: <reason>', which Controller records in Task history. A creation interrupted at the start step reconciles by observing libvirt: running means started, otherwise that outcome reads 'interrupted before the automatic start completed'. A failed or uncertain creation never boots. Controller issues no follow-on power request. Must be false in an edit."
    },
    "autostart": {
      "type": "boolean",
      "description": "libvirt autostart, set while the stopped guest is defined so the host starts this VM on boot. It is not a start."
    },
    "revision": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 0,
      "description": "Definition revision: 0 at creation, current + 1 for each edit. Core refuses any other value, so a stale edit after Controller replacement fails instead of applying an old definition. Reported back in Inventory.managed.vms with the current definition."
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "id",
    "pool_id",
    "host_id",
    "name",
    "description",
    "guest_os",
    "cpu",
    "memory",
    "disks",
    "nics",
    "boot",
    "media_id",
    "start_after_create",
    "autostart",
    "revision"
  ],
  "x-go-type": "protocol.VMCreateSpec",
  "description": "The complete VM specification: Controller-assigned UUID and pool membership with separate host assignment, identity, CPU, memory, every disk and network interface, boot and install media, and the two lifecycle switches. Creation defines a stopped VM and never boots it. Dependencies must have succeeded on this host and pool. Both sides validate the shared bounds through protocol.ValidateVMSpec; Controller owns identity, reference existence, pool-wide MAC uniqueness and capacity."
}

Exact Core outcomes update matching nonterminal Controller operations; a replacement Controller adopts unknown operations as passive rows (actor system, request_id adopt-outcome:<host>:<operation_id>) and never redispatches them. The maximum covers 10000 execution journal entries, 10000 derived start results and 10000 additional retained agent commands; duplicate operation IDs are removed by Core."
